The full-dossier ceiling was 40 and nine full dossiers were used (four Tier-A people and five E-A entities); no report-size overflow occurred. The discovery loop stopped on `budget_exhausted`, with 18 named non-lineage entity branches plus the two lineage registry/founder branches declared above.

## 6. Provenance & Next Data Request

- The register contains 34 filing-supplied and 13 independently discovered subjects; no user-supplied subject. The independent count is mostly the disclosed 2007 lineage and the tier-5 group graph, not an undisclosed-matter count.
- Independent/nominee directorships excluded from beneficial-interest scope: Lloyd Carney, Kermit Crawford, Francisco Fernández-Carbajal, Teri List, John Lundgren, Denise Morrison, Pamela Murphy, William Ready, Linda Rendle, Maynard Webb and Ramon Laguarta. Each remains in the integrity sweep.
- Aggregator conflicts: same-name hits for Ryan McInerney (DOJ's former Hamtramck police officer) and Chris Suh (non-Visa records) were kept in scope, checked against employer/role identifiers, and excluded as namesakes rather than silently treated as clean or adverse. No unresolved roster-mirror conflict was found.
- **Single highest-value next data request:** an up-to-date PACER docket report for *Cai v. Visa Inc.*, No. 24-cv-08220-NW, including any post-January-2026 amended complaint or final disposition.

## 7. People & Network Integrity Read

The register has 11 Clean and 13 Minor-concerns people, with no Material or Disqualifying grade. The riskiest current people are McInerney, Suh and Andreski because a federal securities complaint named them; on 2025-12-10 the court dismissed it with leave to amend for deficient loss causation and did not rule on falsity or intent. [Cai v. Visa Inc., N.D. Cal. order pp.9–11, 2025-12-10]

The discovery loop reached hop 1 and reconciled the 1958/1976 history to the 2007 listed parent; it found no undisclosed lineage, phoenix pattern or controller-linked brand owner. It stopped with named entity/registry gaps, so this is a coverage-limited integrity read capped at 65, not a network clearance. No §24 Filter 1 signal remains open against a Visa officer: the DOJ Ryan McInerney record was a separate, identified person, while the Cai/Kaur cases are civil allegations with no adjudicated misconduct in the record reviewed.

## management-governance / 08_audit-and-assurance-quality.md

# Audit & Assurance Quality — V

Visa Inc. is a U.S. GAAP, NYSE-listed issuer with a September fiscal year-end. `data/V/` is a logical citation label for the frozen evidence generation. The upstream disqualifier scan says **“No disqualifier triggered”** and `Any disqualifier triggered: N`; this report does not re-decide that lock. [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, Verdict-Lock Signal]

## 1. The Assurance Stack

| Role | Firm / Person | Network | Signing partner | Appointed | Tenure (yrs) | Local cap | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Statutory auditor | KPMG LLP (PCAOB Firm ID 185) | KPMG International | Not identified in the admitted filing package; PCAOB Form AP lookup could not be completed | 2007 | 18 completed fiscal years through FY25 | U.S.: no mandatory firm rotation; lead-partner rotation must be tested separately, but partner identity/tenure are not available | [FY25 Form 10-K, p.58; FY26 DEF 14A, p.90; PCAOB firm summary, KPMG LLP (185), 2026-09-23] |
| Principal component auditors | Not disclosed | Not assessable | Not disclosed | N/A | N/A | Four significant subsidiaries are named, but the report gives no component-auditor names or coverage percentages | [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.56–58; Exhibit 21.1] |
| Internal auditor | Visa internal-audit function; individual/firm not disclosed | N/A | N/A | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | U.S. issuer; no prescribed external internal-auditor rotation rule identified | [FY26 DEF 14A, Audit and Risk Committee—Key Activities in 2025] |
| Secretarial auditor (India) | Not Applicable — U.S. issuer | N/A | N/A | N/A | N/A | MR-3/secretarial-audit regime does not apply | [Governance Data Triage—Governance Usability Check, FY25 10-K / FY26 DEF 14A]

The named audit-committee chair is Kermit R. Crawford. Visa discloses an internal-audit function and a Chief Auditor attending regular Audit and Risk Committee risk discussions, but does not name the function leader or provider. [FY26 DEF 14A, Audit and Risk Committee—Key Activities in 2025; Board’s Role in Risk Oversight]

## 2. Opinions, Controls & Restatements (A4-05, A4-09, A7-02)

| FY | Opinion (standalone) | Opinion (consolidated) | EoM / going concern | IFC/ICFR opinion | Restatement / prior-period adjustment | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY25 | Not Applicable — Visa’s U.S. filing presents consolidated statements, not a separate standalone statutory audit | Unqualified: KPMG says the statements present fairly in all material respects | No emphasis-of-matter or going-concern paragraph identified in the FY25 report | Unqualified/effective ICFR at 2025-09-30 | 10-K cover marks no correction of an error to previously issued financial statements and no associated clawback recovery analysis | Green for the current filed audit | [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.56–58; cover page] |
| FY24 | Separate FY24 audit report not in pool | FY24 balance sheet and FY24 operations are included in KPMG’s FY25 opinion | Separate FY24 report not in pool | Separate FY24 ICFR report not in pool | No five-year restatement trail in pool | Insufficient Data for a separate annual-opinion check | [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.56–58] |
| FY23 | Separate FY23 audit report not in pool | FY23 operations are included in KPMG’s FY25 opinion | Separate FY23 report not in pool | Separate FY23 ICFR report not in pool | No five-year restatement trail in pool | Insufficient Data for a separate annual-opinion check | [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.56–58] |

KPMG’s FY25 opinion covers the three fiscal years ended 2025, but this is not the same as reading three separate annual audit reports. The current opinion and ICFR attestation are unqualified. The FY25 Form 10-K cover also answers “no” to an error correction/restatement recovery analysis; that is not proof of no restatement in the full five-year A7-02 window. Visa has a clawback policy, but no recovery can be evaluated because no qualifying restatement is identified in the reviewed disclosures. [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.56–58 and cover page; FY26 DEF 14A, Clawback Policy]

## 3. Calibre, Rotation & Regulator Discipline (A4-01, A4-02, A4-13)

| Test | Raw value | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A4-01 — tenure vs local cap; rotation real? | KPMG has served since 2007, or 18 completed fiscal years through FY25. U.S. issuer: firm rotation is not required; the relevant lead-partner rotation cannot be tested because the partner is not identified. | Terms compliant; rotation orderly and real | Same-network affiliate swap, or cap dodged via loopholes | Amber — firm continuity is disclosed and no firm-change dodge is visible, but the lead-partner test is not assessable | [FY25 Form 10-K, p.58; FY26 DEF 14A, p.90] |
| A4-02 — firm calibre vs company complexity | PCAOB-registered KPMG LLP audits Visa’s global consolidated statements. However, the SEC’s 2019 final order against KPMG imposed a $50m penalty and a cease-and-desist order for misconduct involving PCAOB inspection information and training exams. The PCAOB’s 2023 inspection reported 15 of 58 selected audits in Part I.A; Visa was not named and the sample is not representative of all KPMG audits. | Big-4 / peer-reviewed national firm, listed practice sized to this company | Firm too small for the complexity, or a firm/partner with a disciplinary history | Red — capability is commensurate, but historical firm discipline prevents a clean calibre finding | [SEC Release 34-86118 / AAER-4051, 2019-06-17; PCAOB Release 104-2024-109, pp.1–5] |
| A4-13 — NFRA / PCAOB / ICAI orders vs firm or signing partner | KPMG LLP (185) is currently PCAOB-registered. The SEC’s 2019 order is against KPMG LLP itself. The FY25 Visa signing partner is not identified, so that individual could not be swept; the PCAOB Form AP page did not resolve in the available access path. | No orders against either | Any active order or debarment (also RF-AUD-007) | Red, coverage-limited — firm enforcement history is established; whether any order obligation remains open at the as-of date and the current partner’s record are not proven from available data | [SEC Release 34-86118 / AAER-4051, 2019-06-17; PCAOB firm summary, KPMG LLP (185), 2026-09-23] |

The 2019 SEC order is against KPMG LLP, not Visa or an identified Visa engagement partner. That qualifier matters. It nonetheless defeats any assertion that the audit firm is discipline-free and triggers RF-AUD-007 under the module registry.

## 4. Economic Independence — Fees (A4-06, A4-07)

| FY | Audit fee | Non-audit fee (incl. network firms) | Non-audit / audit (%) | Prohibited services? | Fee vs peer median | Revenue growth | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---:|---|---|
| FY25 | $11.280m | $2.903m = $2.381m audit-related + $0.020m tax + $0.502m all other | 25.7% | No prohibited service identified in the disclosure. Audit-related work was internal-controls reviews/IT attestations; tax work was compliance/planning; all-other work was compliance-related services and financial-statement translation. These labels do not prove prohibited SOX §201 services. | Not assessable — no matched peer-fee evidence in pool | 11.3% | Amber — the all-non-audit-category ratio is just above the <25% Green band; the table does not explicitly state whether every network-firm fee is included | [FY26 DEF 14A, p.91; FY25 Form 10-K, p.60] |
| FY24 | $11.427m | $2.543m = $2.134m audit-related + $0.374m tax + $0.035m all other | 22.3% | No prohibited service identified in the disclosure | Not assessable — no matched peer-fee evidence in pool | 10.0% | Amber — below the Green ratio threshold on this calculation, but peer-level/ network-inclusion evidence is absent | [FY26 DEF 14A, p.91; FY25 Form 10-K, p.60] |

The FY25 all-non-audit-category calculation is `$2.903m / $11.280m = 25.7%`; the FY24 calculation is `$2.543m / $11.427m = 22.3%`. Audit fees fell 1.3% in FY25 while revenue rose 11.3%, which does not meet the red low-balling test of a fee decline alongside revenue growth above 20%. Visa states that all FY25 KPMG services were pre-approved and that the committee considered the tax-service effect on independence. That supports process, not a conclusion that no prohibited service or unreported network fee exists. [FY26 DEF 14A, p.91; FY25 Form 10-K, p.60]

KPMG audits Visa Inc. and subsidiaries on a consolidated basis, but gives no principal-auditor share or other-auditor percentages. | ≥80% | <50%, or cash/revenue-rich components at unknown firms | Amber — coverage cannot be measured | [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.56–58; Exhibit 21.1] |

Material subsidiaries exist and no component-auditor disclosure is available in the pool. This is an Amber disclosure and coverage finding, not evidence that components were unaudited or that an unknown firm was used.

## 6. Resignations & Gatekeeper Stability (A4-04, A4-15)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2007–FY25 | No statutory-auditor firm change identified: KPMG says it has served since 2007 | No firm resignation identified | N/A | Not Applicable — U.S. SEC regime, not SEBI limited-review circular | N/A | KPMG reappointed for FY26 | [FY25 Form 10-K, p.58; FY26 DEF 14A, p.90] |
| 2023-07 | Chris Suh joined Visa as CFO; predecessor exit, reason and timing are not in the admitted source | Not assessable | Not disclosed in reviewed material | Not Applicable — U.S. regime | Not assessable | Current CFO named; he was still presenting for Visa in 2026 | [FY26 DEF 14A, Executive Officers; CIQ Key Developments, 2026-05-19 — vendor export] |
| FY25–Q3 FY26 | Corporate Secretary Kelly Mahon Tullier and internal-audit function are currently disclosed; a three-year change log is absent | Not assessable | Not assessable | Not Applicable — U.S. regime | Not assessable | Current officers/function disclosed | [FY26 DEF 14A, Executive Officers; Audit and Risk Committee—Key Activities in 2025] |

No mid-term audit-firm resignation is identified; A4-04 is Green on the firm-change evidence. A4-15 is **Insufficient Data**: the available documents do not provide a complete rolling 12-month timeline of CFO, corporate-secretary and internal-audit departures, so they cannot establish the absence of an RF-AUD-006 exit cluster.

## 7. KAM / CAM Recurrence (A4-10)

| KAM / CAM | Years it appeared | Underlying balance trend | Sector-standard or company-specific? | Verdict | Source |

| Assessment of the Interchange MDL—Individual Merchant Actions litigation accrual | FY25 only in the admitted annual-audit package | $2.698bn litigation accrual at 2025-09-30; prior-year CAM reports are not in pool | Company-specific litigation estimate | Amber — material current CAM, but recurrence for three years cannot be tested | [FY25 Form 10-K, pp.57–58; Notes 5 and 20] |

The CAM does not by itself modify KPMG’s opinion. It identifies a large, uncertain litigation estimate that required complex judgment. The red recurrence test is not met because historical CAM disclosures were not admitted.

## 10. Read

**Mixed — watch the named link.** Visa’s current consolidated audit and ICFR opinion are unqualified, the fee table is detailed, and the committee reports direct engagement with KPMG. The weakest link is not a Visa audit qualification: it is KPMG LLP’s documented 2019 SEC enforcement order, compounded by the inability to identify and sweep Visa’s engagement partner and to measure component-auditor coverage. To trust the numbers more, obtain the FY25 PCAOB Form AP, a component-auditor/coverage schedule, and three prior annual audit reports/CAMs.

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |

# Related-Party & Group Forensics — V

**Scope.** Visa Inc. (NYSE: V) is a U.S. GAAP, U.S. SEC registrant with a September fiscal year-end. This review uses only the frozen evidence set. The FY25 proxy discloses the related-person policy but no named transaction or amount ledger; therefore a reported zero is **not** evidence of zero economic activity.

## 1. RPT Intensity & the Applicable Regime (A5-01)

Applicable materiality rule per period: U.S. SEC Regulation S-K Item 404(a). It requires disclosure of a registrant transaction exceeding $120,000 in which a related person has a direct or indirect material interest, including the related person, relationship, amount and, for debt, peak balance and terms. The rule applies to the last fiscal year / current proposals; it is not the India LODR regime. [17 CFR 229.404(a), eCFR current to 2026-09-21, accessed 2026-09-23]

| FY | Aggregate RPTs ex-dividends | % of revenue | % of PAT | % of assets | Material-RPT line that year | Above the line? | Minority-approved? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY23 | Not disclosed; no standalone Item 404 / RPT transaction disclosure in the frozen pool | Not assessable | Not assessable | Not assessable | U.S. Item 404 $120k | Not assessable | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [Governance Data Triage — V, §3; 17 CFR 229.404(a), accessed 2026-09-23] |
| FY24 | Not disclosed; no standalone Item 404 / RPT transaction disclosure in the frozen pool | Not assessable | Not assessable | Not assessable | U.S. Item 404 $120k | Not assessable | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [Governance Data Triage — V, §3; 17 CFR 229.404(a), accessed 2026-09-23] |
| FY25 | Not disclosed. The proxy gives the policy only; it does not identify an Item 404 counterparty or amount. FY25 revenue was $40,000m, but there is no numerator to divide by it. | Not assessable | Not assessable | Not assessable | U.S. Item 404 $120k | Not assessable | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [FY25 DEF 14A, p.42, Certain Relationships and Related Person Transactions; earnings/01_historical-financials, §1] |

The RPT ratio and the >25% hard-lock test are **not assessable** from transaction-level disclosure. `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` reports 0.0% only as *reported* RPT sales/purchases and explicitly says this is not proof that activity is zero; it did not trigger the hard lock. This report defers to that owner and does not re-adjudicate it. [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, §1 and §3]

The absence of a named Item 404 row is evidence that Visa did not disclose a reportable related-person transaction under that rule; it is not a complete intra-group or below-threshold RPT ledger and cannot supply the aggregate ratio requested here. [FY25 DEF 14A, p.42; 17 CFR 229.404(a), accessed 2026-09-23]

## 3. Promoter-Linked Channels

### 3A. Royalty / brand / technology fees (A5-02)

| Payee | What is provided | Amount | % of turnover | Basis disclosed? | Trend vs margins | Prior minority approval? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| No controller-linked or external brand payee identified | Visa says it owns and manages the Visa brand and portfolio; historic Visa U.S.A./Visa International trademark roles sit within the disclosed reorganization chain. | Not disclosed | Not applicable | No external licence fee is identified because the available evidence places the core brand inside the consolidated group. | Not assessable | Not applicable under U.S. Item 404; no transaction disclosed | Green on ownership structure; fee quantification not applicable to a controller-linked payee | [FY25 10-K, Item 1—Intellectual Property; people-integrity-dossiers, §0A and §3B] |

This is not a finding that all intra-group charges are zero. It is a finding that the evidence does not show a promoter/controller or outside owner extracting a brand fee. RF-NET-004 does not propagate from 07. [people-integrity-dossiers, §7 and Universal Findings Table, 07-034]

## 4. Round-Tripping Cross-Year Name Match (A5-04)

One list was built from every supplied relationship-graph edge in its stated two-year disclosure window and normalized across customer and supplier roles. The intersection was **0 names**. No named internal group entity appears as the outside counterparty in the graph. This is a two-year vendor-graph check, not a three-to-five-year filing ledger, because no such RPT ledger is in the pool.

| Name | Years as customer | Years as vendor | Both roles? | Sales ≈ purchases (circular)? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No matched name in the supplied graph | Recently disclosed relationships only; exact years not supplied per edge | Recently disclosed relationships only; exact years not supplied per edge | No (0 normalized-name matches) | Not applicable; no two-way name match or values | Green within the limited graph; not a full RPT circularity clearance | [Capital IQ Suppliers/Customers relationship graph, customers/suppliers edges and scope notes, retrieved 2026-09-23 — vendor export] |

No match means there is no circularity lead in this limited source. It does not establish that round-tripping is impossible outside the graph's two-year, recently-disclosed relationship coverage.

## 9. Cash & Funding Topology (A11-02, A11-03)

**Matched-basis rule.** The FY25 reported balance-sheet figures are consolidated, period-end amounts. They show the total cash/debt position but cannot be used to infer a parent-versus-subsidiary cash share; no unmatched ratio is presented.

| Question | Raw value | Basis (both sides) | Matched ratio | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Where does consolidated cash sit (parent vs subs)? | $17,164m consolidated cash equivalents; parent/sub split not disclosed | Period-end consolidated cash; no subsidiary-side comparator | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [earnings/01_historical-financials, §1, citing FY25 10-K pp.59–60] |
| Share held with a related finance company / treasury vehicle | No related financial counterparty identified | N/A | Not assessable | Not Applicable (no data) | [FY25 DEF 14A, p.42; people-integrity-dossiers, §3] |
| Parent borrowing while subs hold the cash? | $25,171m total debt and $17,164m cash equivalents; strict net debt $8,007m | Both totals are FY25 period-end consolidated balances | $8,007m strict net debt; not a parent/sub cash ratio | Insufficient Data on trapped cash | [earnings/01_historical-financials, §1] |
| ICDs to group entities — amount, rate, tenor | No loan/deposit schedule supplied | N/A | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [FY25 DEF 14A, p.42; FY25 10-K, Note 1—Consolidation and basis of presentation] |
| Evergreen / rolled-over ICDs? | Not disclosed | N/A | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [FY25 DEF 14A, p.42] |

No related financial entity is identified, so no equity interest, regulator, approved cap, utilisation, or independent-director/auditor review can be reported. This is an absence of an identified counterparty, not proof that no internal treasury arrangements exist.

## 14. Read

No annual dollar amount of value moving to a promoter or related person is disclosed, so value leakage and its percentage of FY25 PAT are **not assessable**; reported zeros must not be promoted into economic zeros. The only identified channel is a policy-governed but unquantified related-person process, while Visa's core brand is evidenced as group-owned rather than controller-owned. Four significant subsidiaries are disclosed and the vendor graph identifies 10 internal group entities, but neither source maps all entity layers, cash location or intra-group funding. The highest-value next disclosure is a transaction-level Item 404 / related-party ledger with every counterparty, annual amount, approval date, pricing basis and any loan/guarantee peak balance.

## Sweep Log

No additional counterparty registry, court, regulator, or exchange-database lookup was run by this specialist. This report makes no "swept and clean" claim. It relies on the frozen filings, vendor graph and 07's separately logged network work.

## Hard Self-Check

- [x] All 17 owned checklist items (A5-01…A5-10 and A11-01…A11-07) are in the Universal Findings Table.
- [x] The customer-versus-vendor match used all relationships supplied in the graph's stated two-year scope; it is labelled as narrower than a multi-year RPT ledger.
- [x] Slab slicing is recorded as untestable because per-counterparty values are absent.
- [x] The U.S. Item 404 regime is named; India LODR slabs were not applied.
- [x] Policy language was not treated as transaction-level arm's-length proof.
- [x] Available 10-K/proxy M&A disclosures were checked; the unprovided exchange archive limitation is explicit.
- [x] The >25% lock is deferred to `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`.
- [x] Group guarantees are not double counted with A7a-06.
- [x] Every 07-derived entity in scope is classified in Section 13; no RF-PPL-005 / RF-NET-005 fires without a named obligation and amount.
- [x] Cash topology uses only matched period-end consolidated figures and does not infer a parent/sub ratio.
- [x] The score is explicitly inverted; missing transaction data is not scored as zero risk.
- [x] No RPT/leakage red-flag ID is triggered by the available evidence.

## management-governance / 10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md

_Source: `10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md`_

# Contingent Liabilities & Commitments — V

Visa Inc. reports in USD under U.S. GAAP. Amounts below are USD millions unless stated otherwise; fiscal years end September 30. The latest balance-sheet date is June 30, 2026. No external court or tribunal sweep was run for this specialist report, so every litigation-posture statement below is filings-only.

## 1. Headline Exposure (A7a-01)

| Measure | Amount | Denominator (figure + source) | % | Band (Green <5% NW / Red >25% NW or >50% PAT) | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Total contingent liabilities — recognised litigation plus estimated gross unrecognised tax benefits | $3,005 | Net worth = total equity $35,178 at June 30, 2026 [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Consolidated Balance Sheets, p.4] | 8.5% | Amber: above 5%, below 25% | Amber; RF-FIN-003 (High) | Total supplied by [analyses/V_2026-09-23/balance-sheet-survival/05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md, §4], using $1,274 accrued litigation [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 16, p.24] plus $1,731 estimated gross unrecognised tax benefits [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 19, p.98; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 15, p.23] |
| Total contingent liabilities — same recognised measure | $3,005 | FY2025 PAT (net income) = $20,058 [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.60] | 15.0% | Below 50% PAT Red threshold | Amber | Same as above; this is a recognised-contingency measure, not a maximum-loss estimate |
| Total commitments — sponsorship and software | $820 | Net worth = total equity $35,178 at June 30, 2026 [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Consolidated Balance Sheets, p.4] | 2.3% | No CL band applies; separate contractual commitment | Amber | [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 14 (Commitments)] |

The $3,005 total is the balance-sheet-survival module's quantified contingency measure and is not recomputed here. It excludes leases and must not be added to the $168,600 historical maximum daily settlement exposure: the latter is a peak one-day gross unsettled-payment flow, not an estimated loss or a point-in-time liability. [analyses/V_2026-09-23/balance-sheet-survival/05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md, §§1–4]

## 2. Item-by-Item Register & Movement (A7a-02, A7a-13)

| Item (as named in the note) | FY-3 | FY-2 | FY-1 | FY0 | Additions / Reversals / Crystallized | Posture change (forum, last outcome) | Explained in narrative? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Accrued litigation, total | $1,751 | $1,727 | $3,033 | $1,274 at Jun. 30, 2026 | FY25: $2,584 provision and $1,278 payments. 9M FY26: $1,383 provision and $3,142 payments. The lower FY0 balance therefore reflects payments exceeding new provision, not an end to claims. | U.S. covered and European matters remain active; loss ranges outside accrual are not estimable. | Yes — each period reports opening balance, provision, payments and close. | Amber | [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 20, pp.99–100; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 16, p.24] |
| U.S. covered interchange litigation accrual | $1,621 | $1,537 | $2,698 | $822 at Jun. 30, 2026 | FY25: $2,210 provision, $1,049 payments. 9M FY26: $1,131 provision, $3,007 payments. | MDL 1720: 2026 amended injunctive settlement moved to final approval; individual-merchant settlements covered about 95% of opt-out Visa-branded sales volume. Three merchants also sought partial summary judgment over the settlement release. | Yes | Amber | [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 20, pp.99–102; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 16, pp.24–25] |
| VE territory covered litigation accrual | $110 | $72 | $9 | $89 at Jun. 30, 2026 | FY25: $22 provision, $85 payments. 9M FY26: $93 provision, $13 payments. The $80 increase from FY25 is sharp but only 0.2% of June equity. | UK CAT found certain interchange rates restrict competition in June 2025; Visa obtained permission to appeal in March 2026. More than 100 merchant claims remained outstanding. | Yes | Amber | [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 20, pp.100–103; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 16, pp.25–27] |
| Gross unrecognised tax benefits | $3,497 | $3,750 | $1,672 | $1,731 estimated at Jun. 30, 2026 | FY25 net decrease $2,078, primarily from conclusion of an audit; 9M FY26 gross balance increased $59. No silent reversal is disclosed. | IRS deductions dispute is in the U.S. Court of Federal Claims; California refund claims are under administrative appeal; India tax assessments are under appeal. Amounts are not allocated by forum. | Yes | [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 19, pp.98–99; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 15, p.23] |
| Sponsorship and software arrangements | Not in admitted historical commitment table | Not in admitted historical commitment table | $1,147 at Sep. 30, 2025 | $820 entered in July 2026, through FY2035 | The $820 is a later arrangement. The filing does not say whether it replaces any portion of the FY25 schedule, so the two totals are not added. | Contractual commitment; no litigation posture. | Yes for each disclosed schedule; cross-period bridge incomplete. | Amber | [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18, p.95; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 14 (Commitments)] |

The flat-total trap is not present: the large litigation balance fell because Visa disclosed $3,142 of 9M FY26 payments, while the smaller VE balance worsened from $9 to $89 as it disclosed $93 of new provision. The change in VE posture is therefore visible rather than hidden inside the total. The available filing set supports a FY23–FY26 movement read, but does not contain separate FY21–FY22 contingency notes.

## 3. Tax Disputes (A7a-03 direct, A7a-04 indirect)

| Dispute | Type (direct / indirect) | Amount (100%-penalty at face) | Appellate level now | Win/loss record so far | Provided? | % of net worth ($35,178 [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.4]) | Verdict | Source |

## 11. Read

Recognised contingent liabilities are $3.005bn against $35.178bn of June 2026 equity, or 8.5%, so the materiality trigger RF-FIN-003 applies even though the total is below the 25% net-worth Red band. [analyses/V_2026-09-23/balance-sheet-survival/05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md, §4; Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.4] The largest off-P&L item is the settlement indemnification guarantee: $168.6bn maximum daily gross payment exposure, mitigated by $9.5bn of collateral but with no determinable future obligation or loss estimate; it is a scale and liquidity-risk indicator, not $168.6bn of debt. [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 9, p.19] Litigation provisioning appears candid on the available record because Visa shows both accruals and large payments, including $3.007bn paid on U.S. covered litigation in 9M FY26, but an unquantified loss tail and the adverse UK competition finding keep the read Amber. [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 16, pp.24–27] A rational minority holder should watch the year-end litigation accrual and payment bridge, the UK appeal, any quantified loss range, and whether the settlement-collateral pool rises alongside gross exposure.

# Accounting Forensics — V

All amounts are USD millions unless stated. Visa Inc. is a U.S. GAAP payment-network company with a September fiscal year-end, not a bank, NBFC, or insurer. The balance-sheet comparison is FY25 versus FY24; the income statement and cash-flow statement also contain FY23. `earnings/06` and `balance-sheet-survival/01` were present and have been consumed.

## 0. Sector Gate & Inputs

| Item | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Sector (from triage 00) | Payments / Payment Services; U.S. GAAP, USD, September year-end | [business-model/00_data-triage, §2A; Visa FY25 Form 10-K, cover and Note 1] |
| Financials overlay applied? (bank/NBFC/insurer → battery invalid) | No — Visa operates one Payment Services segment, not a lender or insurer. | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, Note 21 — Segment Information] |
| Years of audited annual history in pool | FY23–FY25 operations and CFO; FY24–FY25 audited balance sheets. | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.59–65] |
| earnings/06 baseline present? | Yes — consumed; its FY23–FY25 CFO/GAAP-derived EBITDA read is 94.6%, 81.0%, and 91.5%. | [earnings/06_earnings-quality, §1] |
| balance-sheet-survival/01 debt stack present? | Yes — consumed; FY25 net debt is $8,007 strict basis and net-debt/GAAP-derived EBITDA is 0.32x. | [balance-sheet-survival/01_capital-structure-and-leverage, §§6–7] |
| Battery computable? (needs 2 consecutive annual filings) | Beneish: yes, from audited FY24/FY25 comparative columns. Dechow composite: no — the pool has no FY23 balance-sheet date, required for prior-year cash-sales and ROA changes. | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.59–65] |

## 1. Battery Inputs (two consecutive audited annual filings — every number cited, verbatim per §5)

| Input line item | FY24 | FY25 | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Revenue | $35,926 | $40,000 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.60] |
| Trade receivables | $2,561 | $3,126 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, Consolidated Balance Sheets, p.59] |
| Cost of goods sold / gross margin | $778 network-and-processing expense proxy / 97.83% | $894 network-and-processing expense proxy / 97.77% | Visa does not report COGS or gross profit; the audited network-and-processing line is used solely as the consistently named direct-cost proxy: `(revenue − network and processing) / revenue`. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.60] |
| Current assets | $34,033 | $37,766 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Net PP&E | $3,824 | $4,236 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Securities / non-current investments | $5,745 ($3,200 current + $2,545 non-current) | $2,832 ($1,833 current + $999 non-current) | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Total assets | $94,511 | $99,627 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Depreciation & amortization | $1,034 | $1,220 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.60, 65] |
| SG&A | $10,057 | $11,330 | Build: personnel + marketing + professional fees + G&A; excludes network/processing, D&A, and litigation. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.60] |
| Long-term debt | $20,836 | $19,602 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Current liabilities | $26,517 | $35,048 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| CFO (cash from operations) | $19,950 | $23,059 | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.65] |
| Net income from continuing operations | $19,743 | $20,058 | Visa reports consolidated net income and no discontinued operations; this is therefore the continuing-operations input. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.60] |
| Inventory | Not separately reported | Not separately reported | Visa's primary statements contain no inventory balance or matched COGS line. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.59–60] |
| Cash & bank deposits | $11,975 cash and equivalents | $17,164 cash and equivalents | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Interest income | Not separately disclosed; mixed investment-income line is $962 | Not separately disclosed; mixed investment-income line is $789 | Interest is included in “investment income (expense) and other”; it cannot be scope-matched to cash/deposits. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.60; Note 1] |
| Cash taxes paid | Not separately disclosed in the admitted primary cash-flow statement | Not separately disclosed in the admitted primary cash-flow statement | Do not substitute tax provision for cash tax. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.60, 65] |
| Securities issued during FY25 (equity/debt/convertible/QIP) | — | $3,924 senior-note proceeds; $396 stock issued under equity plans | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.65] |

## 2. Beneish M-Score (A8-14) — computation shown

The M-score is a screening model for patterns common in manipulated accounts; it is not proof of fraud. The calculation was executed from the audited inputs above in the shell. The environment's `python3` launcher was unavailable, so the identical stated formula was evaluated with the available local calculator; no web M-score was used.

| Component | Value FY25 | Non-manipulator mean | Manipulator zone | Verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| DSRI (receivables outrunning sales) | 1.096 | 1.031 | ≥1.465 | Green |
| GMI (gross margin deteriorating) | 1.001 | 1.014 | ≥1.193 | Green |
| AQI (asset quality softening) | 1.021 | 1.039 | ≥1.254 | Green |
| SGI (sales growth pressure) | 1.113 | 1.134 | ≥1.607 | Green |
| DEPI (depreciation rate slowing) | 0.952 | 1.001 | ≥1.077 | Green |
| SGAI (overhead vs sales) | 1.012 | 1.054 | not banded (negative weight in M) | Neutral |
| LVGI (leverage rising) | 1.095 | 1.037 | ≥1.111 | Green |
| TATA (accruals vs assets) | (3.012%) | 0.018 | ≥3.1% | Green |

Calculation: `DSRI=(3,126/40,000)/(2,561/35,926)=1.096`; `GMI=(1−778/35,926)/(1−894/40,000)=1.001`; `AQI=[1−(37,766+4,236+2,832)/99,627]/[1−(34,033+3,824+5,745)/94,511]=1.021`; `SGI=40,000/35,926=1.113`; `DEPI=[1,034/(1,034+3,824)]/[1,220/(1,220+4,236)]=0.952`; `SGAI=(11,330/40,000)/(10,057/35,926)=1.012`; `LVGI=[(19,602+35,048)/99,627]/[(20,836+26,517)/94,511]=1.095`; `TATA=(20,058−23,059)/99,627=−3.012%`. [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.59–65]

Composite: `M = −4.84 + 0.920·1.096 + 0.528·1.001 + 0.404·1.021 + 0.892·1.113 + 0.115·0.952 − 0.172·1.012 − 0.327·1.095 + 4.679·(−0.03012) = −2.461`.

| Composite / rule | Value | Green band | Amber band | Red band | Verdict |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| M-score | -2.461 | < -2.22, no component in zone | -2.22 to -1.78 | > -1.78 | Green |
| Components in manipulator zone | 0 of 7 banded | 0 | 1–2 | ≥3 | Green |

## 3. Dechow F-Score & Accrual Battery (A8-17, A8-18)

The F-score is a model scaled so 1.00 is the average-company misstatement risk. It cannot be calculated honestly: the FY25 10-K provides FY25/FY24 balance sheets but no FY23 balance sheet, so both `ΔCASH_SALES` and `ΔROA` lack their required t−2 inputs. No opening balance was approximated.

| Test | Value | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Dechow F-score (1.00 = average company) | Not computable (t−2 balance sheet unavailable) | <1.00 | ≥1.85 | Insufficient Data | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.59–65] |
| RSST accruals / average assets | Not computed — balance-sheet classification inputs and full F model unavailable | ≤3% | ≥10% | Insufficient Data | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Soft assets % of total assets | 57.84% = `(99,627−4,236−37,766)/99,627` | <50% | >65% and rising | Amber | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Δ receivables / average assets | 0.58% = `(3,126−2,561)/97,069` | <2% | ≥5% | Green | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.59] |
| Δ inventory / average assets | Not computable — inventory not separately reported | <2% | ≥5% | Insufficient Data | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, pp.59–60] |
| A8-18 — issuance in a flagged year | $3,924 debt plus $396 equity-plan proceeds; neither M nor TATA was flagged (`M=-2.461`, `TATA=-3.012%`) | none while flagged | issued while F ≥1.85 or TATA ≥3.1% | Green | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, p.65; Section 2] |

The report describes audit and ICFR procedures but does not state a bank-confirmation procedure. | Red: confirmations not independently obtained | Insufficient Data | [Visa FY25 Form 10-K, Auditor’s Report, pp.56–58] |

## 12. Forensics Read

The computed Beneish M-score is **-2.461**, Green, with zero components in the manipulator zone; FY25 cash also exceeded reported profit (`CFO/PAT 1.15x`) and EBITDA conversion was 91.5%. The most relevant watch item is ordinary receivables: they rose 22.1% while revenue rose 11.3%, taking DSO from 26.0 to 28.5 days — an Amber collections signal, not evidence of paper revenue because the FY25 collections proxy still equalled 98.6% of revenue. The Dechow F-score and cash-yield confirmation tests are not computable from the admitted inputs, so a rational minority holder should treat the books as low measured manipulation risk, not as fully cleared.

# Regulatory, Legal & Compliance — V

**As of:** 2026-09-23  
**Entity and scope:** Visa Inc. (NYSE: V; CIK 0001403161), a U.S. SEC registrant. Five-year direct-entity lookback: 2021-09-23 to 2026-09-23. This report uses the frozen evidence inventory cited logically as data/V/. “No result” means only that the named source search did not produce an attributed item; it is not a clearance unless the sweep log says coverage is complete.

**Bottom line:** **hygiene lapses, watch.** Visa discloses a live DOJ civil antitrust case, an EC preliminary inquiry and large, continuing interchange litigation. The evidence reviewed does not show a material public matter absent from Visa’s own disclosures, but the external sweep is coverage-limited and key hygiene evidence is missing.

**Legal & regulatory risk: 52/100 (INVERTED — higher means worse); confidence 3/5.** This is a risk score, not an investment rating. The main drivers are two unresolved government matters, rather than a proven undisclosed-liability or messenger-retaliation finding.

## 1. Regulator Enforcement Register (A9-01 / A9-02)

The business-model disqualifier scan reports, verbatim, “No disqualifier triggered” and “Any disqualifier triggered: N.” That is an upstream screening result, not a replacement for the legal record below.

| Matter / authority | Type, allegation and current status | Amount / net-worth test | Filed disclosure | A9 verdict / flag |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| U.S. Department of Justice, Antitrust Division — U.S. v. Visa Inc. | DOJ filed a civil complaint on 2024-09-24 alleging Sherman Act monopolization and attempted monopolization in debit-network markets. DOJ’s case page records the case as opened and a June 2025 memorandum opinion; Visa says the court denied its dismissal motion on 2025-06-23. The requested relief is injunctive; no fine or damages amount is stated in the sources reviewed. | Not quantifiable; **High** because an adverse antitrust injunction could affect core debit-network conduct. | Yes — Visa describes the complaint and dismissal-motion outcome. | **Red** — A9-01 enforcement and A9-02 active government matter. RF-REG-001. |
| European Commission | Visa says that on 2024-08-30 the EC informed it of a preliminary investigation into fees Visa charges acquirers, and that Visa is cooperating. The record reviewed gives no charge, finding, timetable or monetary amount. | Not quantifiable; **High**, unquantified. | Yes — described in Note 20. | **Red** — A9-02 active government matter. RF-REG-001. |
| SEC enforcement release / litigation screen | No Visa-attributed enforcement action was retrieved from the accessible SEC enforcement materials. The SEC issuer-specific submissions/API search was unavailable and the generic release page is not a complete issuer case search. | N/A | N/A | **Insufficient Data**, not clean. |
| State regulators / other competition authorities | No complete state-attorney-general or non-U.S. regulator docket sweep was available. Visa’s Note 20 lists private and public legal matters, but that does not prove that no other regulator matter exists. | N/A | N/A | **Coverage-limited.** |

Visa reported a litigation accrual of $1.274bn at 2026-06-30, including $822m of U.S. covered-litigation accrual, versus $35.178bn of equity. The $822m component is 2.3% of equity; the total accrual is 3.6%. These are accounting accruals, not a ceiling on total exposure or a regulator penalty. [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, filed 2026-07-29, Consolidated Balance Sheets p.4; Note 16—Legal Matters, pp.24–28]

### 1A. Predecessor / Lineage Sweep

The people-and-lineage dossier identifies no former name for Visa Inc. and identifies Visa U.S.A. Inc., Visa International Service Association, Visa Canada Corporation/Association, and Inovant LLC as entities that became subsidiaries in the 2007 reorganization. The rows below remain **still-pending** because the external courts/regulator/sanctions sweep is incomplete. No reviewed scheme document affirmatively establishes a transfer of each predecessor’s historical regulatory liability to Visa Inc.; therefore these entities are qualified lineage exposure, not separate direct A9 enforcement findings against Visa Inc. [management-governance/07_people-and-culture-dossier.md, Sections 0 and 3B]

A historic Starpay/VIMachine action was dismissed by stipulated order in 2009; no complete modern docket/regulator screen. | **still-pending — coverage-limited** |

Historic Starpay/VIMachine dismissal found; no complete modern docket/regulator screen. | **still-pending — coverage-limited** |

The historic Starpay/VIMachine and Inovant entries are old resolved/disclosed items, not current enforcement. They matter only because the lineage sweep cannot be represented as clean. [SEC EDGAR exhibit, Stipulated Order of Dismissal, 2009; SEC S-1/A comment-response materials, 2007, Inovant note]

NYSE’s current noncompliant-issuer page did not list Visa when retrieved, but it is a current-page view and does not establish eight quarters of issuer-specific fine history. [NYSE Regulation, Noncompliant Issuers, retrieved 2026-09-23] Therefore **A9-06 is Insufficient Data**. There is no evidence in the reviewed sample of repeated exchange fines, but absence of that evidence is not a green result.

### Filing / control signals — required 12-quarter view

Only two of the required 12 quarterly periods were directly observed in the frozen pool. Visa’s Q3 FY2026 management conclusion says disclosure controls and procedures were effective at reasonable assurance and that there was no material change in ICFR during the quarter. That supports neither a 12-quarter timeliness conclusion nor a full control-history clearance. [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, filed 2026-07-29, Item 4—Controls and Procedures, p.40]

| Restatement / ICFR weakness in reviewed filings | None identified in the two reviewed filings | Narrow-sample observation only |
| Latest disclosure-control conclusion | Effective at reasonable assurance; no material ICFR change | Positive but current-period only |

**A7-01: Insufficient Data.** It has no red-flag trigger on the evidence reviewed, but the required 12-quarter evidence is absent.

The absence of a draw is not evidence about lender quality. [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, filed 2025-11-06, Note 13—Debt, pp.83–84; management-governance/01_disqualifier-scan.md]

## 4. Whistleblower Machinery and Messenger Response

Visa discloses several reporting routes: businessconduct@visa.com, a confidential hotline and web reporting channel at visa.alertline.com, mail, and Board communications. It states that communications are handled under its Whistleblower Policy. The proxy does not disclose received, closed, substantiated or pending complaint statistics, and the reviewed governance material does not identify an ombuds function. [Visa 2026 DEF 14A, filed 2025-12-08, Stockholder Communications with the Board; Code of Business Conduct and Ethics]

A shareholder proposal asked Visa to report on AI-driven online sexual exploitation and cited reporting about adult-platform-related risks. Visa opposed the proposal and described payment-integrity controls, its High Integrity Risk Program, and Board oversight. The reviewed record does not show an independent forensic review of that underlying allegation, nor does it show Visa suing or otherwise attacking the messenger. This is not proof that no investigation occurred; it is a disclosure-limit finding. [Visa 2026 DEF 14A, filed 2025-12-08, Stockholder Proposal—Report on AI-Driven Online Sexual Exploitation, pp.102–105]

| Test | Read | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| A9-03 mechanism and outcome evidence | Multiple channels disclosed; no ombuds or case-statistics evidence | **Amber / Insufficient Data** |
| A9-11 messenger response | No independent forensic review disclosed; no messenger-attack finding in sources reviewed | **Amber**, no RF-CMP-002 triggered |

## 5. Insider and Trading Conduct (A9-07)

No company-attributed insider-trading enforcement action was established from the limited SEC enforcement/release materials and frozen filings reviewed. This is **Insufficient Data**, not a green result: a complete Form 4/PIT-style transaction review and an issuer-specific SEC enforcement query were not available. No person is handed back to the people dossier because no attributed company-insider enforcement event was identified.

## 6. Sanctions (A9-08)

OFAC General License 15C, dated 2020-03-12, expressly authorized certain Visa Inc. transactions involving Banco de Venezuela subject to the license’s terms. It is an authorization, not an OFAC violation or enforcement finding. It is also historical and cannot prove current sanctions controls. Visa’s FY2025 filing says it suspended operations in Russia in March 2022; that is a disclosed operational response, not a full sanctions-screen result. [OFAC General License 15C, 2020-03-12; Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, filed 2025-11-06, Risk Factors / international operations discussion]

No controlled sanction-evasion finding was found in sources reviewed, but direct current OFAC/UN/EU screening and counterparty verification were not completed. **A9-08: Amber / Insufficient Data.**

## 7. Environmental, Consumer, and Product-Compliance Penalties (A9-09)

No primary, issuer-specific environmental, consumer-protection or product-compliance penalty register was retrieved. The DOJ antitrust case belongs in the enforcement register; the shareholder proposal belongs in the messenger-response review; neither is proof of a material environmental or consumer penalty. **A9-09: Insufficient Data.**

## 8. Litigation Reconciliation (A9-10)

| Material public matter identified | Current financial / legal signal | Disclosed in current filing? | Reconciliation |
|---|---|---|---|
| DOJ debit-network civil antitrust suit | Open civil antitrust action; injunctive relief sought; amount not stated | Yes | Reconciled to Note 16 and Note 20 |
| U.S. interchange multidistrict litigation | $822m covered accrual at 2026-06-30; amended settlement preliminarily approved 2026-06-09; individual merchant settlements cover 95% of Visa-branded sales of opting-out merchants. Potential remaining exposure is not quantifiable from the filing. | Yes | Reconciled |
| European interchange merchant actions | More than 1,200 historical merchant claimants; more than 950 settled and more than 100 outstanding; legal outcomes and amounts remain uncertain | Yes | Reconciled |
| EC preliminary acquirer-fee investigation | Preliminary investigation; no charge/fine amount reported | Yes | Reconciled |

No material public court matter located in this sweep is absent from the reviewed Visa legal-matters disclosures. Because U.S. state, foreign court, and comprehensive PACER/RECAP coverage was not available, that is a **coverage-limited reconciliation**, not a definitive clean-litigation conclusion. **A9-10: Amber; RF-CMP-001 not triggered.** [Visa Q3 FY2026 Form 10-Q, filed 2026-07-29, Note 16—Legal Matters, pp.24–28; Visa FY2025 Form 10-K, filed 2025-11-06, Note 20—Legal Matters, pp.99–107]

## 9. Read

The public record reviewed corroborates Visa’s filings rather than contradicting them: the live DOJ case, the EC preliminary inquiry and the major interchange matters are disclosed. The concern is the scale and duration of those unresolved matters, not a proven disclosure omission.

The remaining evidence gap matters. Eight-quarter exchange-fine history, twelve-quarter timeliness, current rating-action history, lender identity/quality, and a complete sanctions/court sweep are not established. Visa has accessible whistleblower channels, but it does not provide complaint outcomes or an identified ombuds, and its response to the published adult-content concern does not show an independent forensic review.

**Conclusion: hygiene lapses, watch.** No hard A9-10 candor gate or critical misconduct finding is triggered on the evidence reviewed. The incomplete sweep sets a legal-regulatory risk floor of 40 and a confidence ceiling of 70 under the module rules; this report applies a 52/100 inverted risk score and 3/5 confidence.
